Hmm wait, but that doesn't align: wrist camera axes are rotated. EEF at (0.0016, 0.2293). Point (600,250) = (0.008,0.332) which is +0.10 in y from the eef; (600,750) = (-0.008,0.131) which is -0.10 in y. Roughly symmetric about y=0.23. Good.

Now what are these points? At table z=0.4248 for (600,750) → that's the table outside the basket (col 750 right side of wrist image = -y direction = toward the table center). And (700,130) → (-0.053, 0.419, 0.4248) — table surface at y 0.42 (outside basket beyond the far side).

Hmm, so at z=0.4248 the world y range for these pixels goes 0.13 to 0.42 — meaning those pixels see the table (basket floor is above the table? no...). Wait the basket sits ON the table; the basket interior floor is around z 0.44-0.46 maybe.

Hmm, (600,250)→z 0.485 y 0.332: that's likely the inner liner surface. 

I think the wrist camera has a wide FOV and sees past the basket.

This is getting confusing. Use the agentview region mode at the current step, excluding the held box, to find the cavity. Actually, a cleaner approach: the basket hasn't moved, so use my earlier agentview-based boundary estimates:
- back rim x=-0.058..-0.067 at y 0.198..0.282
- front rim x=+0.078 at y 0.257
- one point (680,850) → (0.072, 0.188, 0.517)

Hmm, the y-extent: 0.188 to 0.29. If the basket interior spans y 0.19..0.29, center y = 0.24. The x-extent -0.067..+0.078, center x = 0.005. That's what I used. 

Now, wrist data says at the eef xy (0.0, 0.23) we're above the basket... but (600,750) at (0.0,0.131, 0.4248) — the region below/right. Hmm.

Let me instead do an agentview region query for the cavity with a z band that excludes the table (z between 0.44 and 0.56) over the basket pixel window, at the current step (basket visible? yes, right side). Since the eef is now over the basket, part of it is occluded, but the near rim is visible.

Alternative and most robust: descend slowly with small step_clip and let OSC stall — memory (probe-container-floor-by-stall-height) says the stall height tells you the floor. But that risks jamming the box against the rim; the box is small though, and if it descends into the cavity it will stall on the liner floor.

Reference recipe: descend to z 0.57 with step_clip 0.02 and OSC stalled ~0.58, then released → success. So the effective floor is around 0.575 in their case. Here the rim is at z~0.573, so a similar stall ~0.50-0.55 might occur.

I'll descend to target (0.005, 0.235, 0.50) with step_clip 0.015 and let it stall; the stall leaves the box just above the liner floor inside the basket. Then release.

Risk: if the descend goes through the rim, the box might be pressed against the liner and pushed out. Descend slowly and see. Let's do a first descend to z=0.57 and inspect.
